All three installations returned 22,601 characters via pypdf and 42,984 via
pdfplumber, with the expected Summary heading. Async output hashes match sync.
Default selection was pdfplumber and its output matched explicit pdfplumber.
This is capability evidence on one public document, not an extraction-quality test.

All returned empty strings for a valid blank PDF. Missing input raised
FileNotFoundError; unknown backend raised ValueError; missing Docling raised
ImportError. Corrupt input produced PdfStreamError with pypdf and PdfminerException
with pdfplumber. These exception types reflect the tested installed dependencies.
CLI controls include parse/version/discovery/missing/unknown/corrupt/blank/options.
The current CLI lacks the inherited Python configuration options.

Public source and release honor parse(pdf_path=..., backend="pypdf") and reuse a
no-option backend instance. The inherited working build breaks both behaviors.
The inherited pdfplumber best preset raises TypeError for layout; pypdf preset=typo

TestBackendSelection.test_get_backend_returns_singleton. Logs retain the commands.

Additional source observations, not runtime proof
available_backends() scans DEFAULT_PREFERENCE rather than all 19 registry entries.
Several installation hints use backend names that do not match extras (aws, azure,
google), and PyMuPDF4LLM's light-extra hint does not install its own package.
Inherited common/preset mappings exceed several backend constructor contracts.
Docling stores some settings without applying them to its converter. Frontier
constructors accept arbitrary kwargs without using them. Their parse paths ignore
provider truncation/finish status. OpenAI's upload cleanup is only on success.

faithfulness, semantic formatting and visual grounding and publishes code/data.
Those are LlamaIndex's benchmark dimensions, not PDFsmith measurements. PDF-Bench
owns any corpus/metric reuse and experiments. PDFsmith must preserve usable output
and expose its actual configuration without promising a score or universal quality.
Source: https://www.llamaindex.ai/blog/parsebench

The current LlamaParse getting-started guide uses llama-cloud>=2.8 and Parse v2.
Its migration guide requires tier/version, nests processing/output options and
changes page selection to 1-based indexing; v1 remains available but new features
are v2-only. This affects compatibility and request identity. Our current wrapper
imports llama_parse.LlamaParse and accepts parsing_mode without forwarding it.
The plan proposes a v2 migration under the same backend name, explicit legacy
setting treatment and actual SDK transport checks before any live run.

BEGIN FILE spec.md SHA256 46a459510307266124cffd92dd08b02701d9ef40812bebecffe5f18437aa7631
# Spec: Update and verify every registered PDFsmith integration

Alignment brief: `start-here.txt`, commissioned by Stefan on October 14, 2026.
Stefan selected "Verify every registered backend now" in the alignment reply and
requested consideration of LlamaIndex's ParseBench announcement. This replaces
the proposed baseline-only scope. This specification describes the required end
state; implementation requires the returned external reviews specified below.

## Objective

A caller can install, discover, configure and invoke PDFsmith's registered
parsers through a dependable Python API and CLI, identify failures and empty
output, and inspect exactly which integrations have fresh end-to-end evidence.

## Terms

Context: pdfsmith, document-parsing-library

| Term | Readings | Status | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| Backend scope | Two local baselines only; or every currently registered integration | resolved-by-author | Stefan selected verification of every registered backend. All 19 names in the inherited registry are in scope, including LlamaParse. |
| Verified integration | Import or mock test passes; or installed package parses an identified document through the actual integration | resolved-by-author | The brief requires fresh end-to-end runs and explicitly says mocked provider tests do not establish a working paid integration. |
| Available | Registered; dependencies installed; configured; or proven to work on a document | resolved-by-author | The brief requires discovery versus actual availability to be addressed. These states must remain distinguishable; dependency detection cannot assert service readiness. |
| Default selection | A document-specific best-parser decision; or a fixed preference order | resolved-by-author | The brief and public capability packet require truthful fixed-order selection, without parser-quality inference. |
| Failure | Every empty output; or an exception, rejected input/configuration, or provider-declared unsuccessful/incomplete operation | resolved-by-author | The brief requires failure and partial/empty output handling. A valid blank PDF may return an empty string; it must be recorded as empty, not as recovered content. |
| Version identity | Module string alone; or distribution, module, source and backend identities together | resolved-by-author | The brief explicitly requires investigation of pyproject/module disagreement and exact milestone identities. |
| Completion | Plan or passing mocks; or reviewed implementation with declared evidence and unresolved live-service gates visible | resolved-by-author | The brief requires engineering milestones after actual GPT and Claude reviews and separately prohibits unauthorized paid calls. |

## Why

PDF-Bench needs pypdf and pdfplumber on identical inputs, controlled options and
accurate implementation identity. Application callers also need the other
registered integrations to have honest installation and operating contracts.
The old public API, inherited API proposal and working provider changes currently
describe different behavior. A wrapper that accepts an option but ignores it can
invalidate a consumer's experiment without producing an obvious exception.

LlamaParse is an active dependency, and its current API needs assessment rather
than assuming the old wrapper still expresses current service settings.
ParseBench is relevant prior work on document evaluation. Its findings do not
establish the behavior or quality of a PDFsmith integration.

## Acceptance criteria

1. A self-contained specification/implementation packet is reviewed externally
   by GPT and Claude. Retain the actual returned reports, their identities and
   hashes, and a disposition of consequential findings. No substantial source
   implementation starts before those findings are reconciled. Baseline diagnostic
   work is permitted by the brief.
2. Inventory public source, released distribution and inherited working tree
   separately, including source hashes and initial dirty state. Preserve all
   inherited files. Record known chronology separately from unknown authorship;
   do not absorb unrelated files into commits.
3. Account for all 19 existing registry names in one backend verification matrix.
   Each row identifies its install extra and SDK/runtime dependencies, input types,
   supported options, sync/async behavior, configuration, actual test evidence and
   retain/repair/replace disposition. No backend silently disappears or is called
   working solely because it is registered or imports.
4. Every local integration has a fresh isolated installed-package parse of an
   identified public PDF and controlled text/blank/corrupt fixtures, or an exact
   reproduced environment/upstream failure that prevents a support claim. A failure
   remains open engineering work, not a passing verification. Baseline parsers can
   finish their milestones independently of another backend's blocker.
5. Every service integration has current official-API verification, isolated SDK
   and request/response contract checks, missing-configuration and error-path
   checks, and a reproducible live-run protocol. Actual service verification needs
   separately authorized inputs, provider/configuration and spending. Until a real
   run occurs its status remains live-unverified, even if offline tests pass.
6. Preserve `parse(path, backend=...) -> str`, `parse_async(...) -> str`, released
   `pdf_path=` keyword use, backend identifiers, direct loader/constructor/parse
   usage needed by PDF-Bench, and existing CLI parse/output/backend commands.
   Retain no-option `get_backend()` instance reuse unless a reviewed compatibility
   migration explicitly supersedes it. New configuration cannot leak between calls.
7. Explicit selection never silently changes backend. Default selection is
   deterministic for a declared installed environment, stays local/unpaid, reports
   its selected name through the public selection surface, and does not claim
   quality ranking. An unavailable or broken selected integration cannot silently
   substitute a different parser after parsing begins.
8. Discovery distinguishes registry membership, dependency availability,
   configuration and successful parsing. Listing backends cannot initialize models,
   obtain credentials, invoke remote services or create provider resources.
   Installation guidance uses an extra that actually installs the integration.
9. Supported options reach the producing SDK/parser calls. Unknown options, wrong
   types, invalid presets and unsupported common options fail explicitly. Sync and
   async entry points apply the same validation and precedence. Regression evidence
   demonstrates an output-affecting option on a fixture that makes it observable.
   Advertised settings cannot be accepted without effect.
10. Local path/configuration errors occur before parser/model/client initialization
    or remote requests. Missing paths, directories, corrupt inputs, unsupported
    formats, unavailable integrations and invalid options have documented outcomes.
    Input files and existing output files remain unchanged when an operation fails.
11. Backend exceptions and provider-declared partial, failed or truncated responses
    cannot become apparently successful completed output. Valid empty content
    remains distinguishable from an exception, and empty-content evidence is
    reported separately from content recovery. PDFsmith does not guarantee detection
    of silent semantic omissions by an upstream parser.
12. CLI failure returns nonzero, keeps diagnostics on stderr, and preserves any
    previous output on parse, serialization or write failure. Successful file output
    is complete UTF-8 text. CLI options express the supported Python configuration
    contract, with invalid configuration rejected before processing.
13. Async parsing preserves content/errors and allows unrelated loop work to run
    during imports, initialization and blocking extraction. Document cancellation
    boundaries, including work in executor threads that cannot be forcibly stopped.
    Repeated/concurrent calls do not mix options or mutable result accounting.
14. Files, native documents, page handles, streams and temporary remote resources
    have defined cleanup on success and failure. Cleanup failure cannot conceal the
    original parse failure. Remote calls cannot be rolled back; any remaining
    artifacts or uncertain service completion must be reported honestly.
15. The unpublished development build has one consistent PEP 440 version across
    wheel metadata, module and CLI. Record the unchanged public/released 0.2.0
    identity separately. No package release or claim that old artifacts used the
    new implementation follows from this metadata repair.
16. Each completed engineering milestone includes scoped source/package identities,
    exact commands, fresh pass/fail results, backend dependency snapshots, input and
    output hashes, supported behaviors, exclusions and runnable API/CLI examples.
    Regression tests fail on actual reproduced producing-code defects and pass on
    repaired code. Existing failures and lint/type drift encountered are resolved
    within the reviewed scope rather than suppressed.
17. A final installed-wheel exercise runs outside this checkout using only declared
    extras. Core-only import, no-backend behavior and per-extra installation are
    tested. Supported Python/platform bounds reflect observed compatibility; no
    workstation-only model/cache state is silently required.
18. One concise website-editorial handoff supplies verified identities and claim
    boundaries. It does not change website, editorial, PDF-Bench or historical
    experiment files, and it cannot imply universal parser quality or production
    validation from smoke tests.

## In scope

- All registered local backends: pypdf, pdfplumber, pymupdf, pymupdf4llm,
  pdfminer, pypdfium2, unstructured, kreuzberg, extractous, docling and marker.
- All registered service backends: aws_textract, azure_document_intelligence,
  google_document_ai, databricks, llamaparse, anthropic, openai and gemini.
- Python/CLI contracts, optional dependencies, configuration, resource management,
  supported PDF/image capabilities and evidence-based documentation corrections.
- Current LlamaParse API migration assessment, effective mode/tier and version
  controls, plus a dated account of ParseBench's relevance and ownership boundary.

## Out of scope

- PDF-Bench corpus/reference construction, experiments, scores and comparisons.
- Reimplementing ParseBench, claiming its scores as our results, universal quality
  rankings, provider-price tables or performance claims from successful execution.
- New parser integrations beyond the current registry without a separate scope
  decision. LiteParse is a follow-on candidate, not silently added here.
- Paid or free-credit service calls, public-main merge, package release, website
  writes, publication, scheduling and deployment without separate authorization.
- New chats, hidden agents and unrelated inherited-work cleanup that discards work.

## Constraints

- Work in this checkout and work unit. Secrets, `.env`, MCP settings, old prompts
  and credential files cannot enter review packets or Git checkpoints.
- This repository is PUBLIC, verified using `gh repo view`. Use scoped branches;
  branch pushes are allowed, public-main merge is not authorized.
- Use uv and repository checks. Isolate incompatible parser dependency stacks;
  an advertised bundle must resolve, or its compatibility limits must be explicit.
- Actual external reviews are a required dependency. A locally written review
  prompt or an internal self-review does not satisfy that dependency.
- Full live-service verification can remain blocked on Stefan's separate run
  authorization. It cannot be represented as complete offline verification.

## Environment / prior art

- Public main is `fe66170231dd580d9f919662ed3b89947f091763`, confirmed against
  `git ls-remote`; PyPI's current release is 0.2.0. The inherited module says 0.3.0
  while `pyproject.toml` says 0.2.0. `baseline/` records fresh installation evidence.
- Seven tracked modifications predate this work: module/API, four provider files
  and uv.lock. The December 14, 2025 API proposal matches the API modification
  chronology; provider modification timestamps are March 10, 2026. Authors are
  unknown from Git. These are preservation facts, not approved implementation.
- `CLAUDE.md` references `.claude/PROJECT_MAP.md`, generated November 2025.
  Its backend counts, quality claims and API line references are historical.
- Registry loaders and backend constructors already support PDF-Bench's direct
  adapter use. PDF-Bench's current reconstruction spec requires pypdf/pdfplumber
  first and independently owns caching, reference truth and attempt accounting.
- The existing README correction is remote branch
  `codex/public-capability-copy-2026-10-09`, commit
  `cb5094e6b2690b50da748bcea4875adaa8c74dfc`. Preserve and reconcile it rather
  than duplicating or overwriting that work.
- [ParseBench announcement](https://www.llamaindex.ai/blog/parsebench), published
  April 13, 2026, and [LlamaParse migration guide](https://developers.llamaindex.ai/llamaparse/parse/guides/migration-v1-to-v2/),
  checked for this plan. Benchmark claims remain attributed to their authors.

## Existing surface (must not break)

- Released Python string API, keyword compatibility, backend names and CLI syntax.
- PDF-Bench loader/constructor use and access to the backend `name` attribute.
- Existing useful inherited API/provider work, configuration files, historical
  review artifacts and the independent README correction branch.
- Historical evidence remains byte-for-byte historical. New results name their
  actual package, backend versions, options and inputs.

## Open questions

- External design reviews are not yet returned. Stefan supplies the reports; this
  owner resolves consequential findings before the affected implementation.
- Live-service runs require Stefan's exact provider/configuration/input and spend
  authorization. Prepare concrete protocols after offline repair; no implicit use
  of existing credentials or free credits.
- Dependency/model/runtime incompatibilities must be reproduced per backend.
  This owner repairs them against current official APIs after review; an upstream
  or machine-only blocker gets exact evidence and a support exclusion, not a pass.
- Existing-work authorship is unknown. Preserve and hash the inherited bytes;
  review and attribute integration decisions without inventing author provenance.

## Next actions

Stefan returns GPT and Claude reviews of the completed packet before substantial
implementation. Live-provider spending remains a separate later decision.

BEGIN FILE plan.md SHA256 0fc2b25543344051e39d965061e6de063a50ab37843f345af1aedc934b5dc57b
# Plan: PDFsmith library update and complete backend verification

This plan decomposes spec.md after Stefan selected every registered backend for
verification. It is a proposal for external GPT and Claude review, not evidence
that implementation or service validation occurred. All source changes depend on
returned reports and reconciliation of consequential findings. Baseline diagnostics
are already authorized and are recorded separately under baseline/.

## Design for review

Preserve the released string API and backend identifiers. Repair the inherited
extension rather than replacing the parser architecture. Keep registry metadata
separate from initialized instances: discovery must not create clients/models.
Expose a full registered inventory and distinguish dependency detection from
configured/readiness and fresh document-level verification. Proposed public
addition: registered_backends() -> list[BackendInfo]. Keep the existing no-argument
available_backends() order for compatibility; add an explicit include_services
keyword if needed to inspect installed service dependencies without initializing
them. Reviewers should challenge whether this addition is sufficient or redundant.

Resolve one backend per operation, then validate effective options against its
explicit supported contract before constructing it. Unknown keys and unsupported
preset/OCR requests fail rather than disappear. Do not infer support from **kwargs.
Retain no-option get_backend() reuse; configured parse calls must not share mutable
option dictionaries or result accounting. Run blocking setup/extraction off the
async event loop; use a native async path only where its setup and errors obey the
same contract. Document cancellation of executor work without claiming it kills a
thread or rolls back a provider job.

Use a single development-version source for an unpublished 0.3.0.dev0 build; the
existing public wheel remains 0.2.0. The reviewed implementation may use Hatch's
version-from-module support. The inherited 0.3.0 string is evidence of a proposal,
not authority to release. No release is part of this plan.

For local options, forward only documented settings to the producing extraction
calls, with per-backend schemas and behavior tests. Preserve default output unless
a reproduced defect requires a reviewed correction. Retain preset names only as
configuration aliases, without a quality guarantee. Keep images backend-specific;
reject formats not actually supported by that wrapper. Do not promise structured
JSON, bounding boxes or semantic completeness through a str return value.

For services, test the real installed SDK with offline transports and official
response shapes, not a second implementation of the wrapper. Validate paths and
options before uploads, inspect failure/truncation/partial statuses, use bounded
polling, and clean up owned temporary resources on failure. Offline SDK success is
not a live integration pass. Service E2E remains an explicit pending gate until
Stefan approves a concrete provider/configuration/input/spend protocol.

LlamaParse is an early independent service milestone. Assess migrating the existing
llamaparse backend to the current llama-cloud Parse v2 SDK, with explicit tier and
service version, nested processing/output controls and observable cache policy.
Keep its identifier, and provide an explicit legacy parsing_mode compatibility
mapping or rejection. A premium-to-agentic_plus mapping cannot be assumed equivalent.
The current wrapper does not forward parsing_mode at all. ParseBench informs the
need for preserved table/format content and transparent configuration; comparisons
and benchmark reuse belong to PDF-Bench. Adding LiteParse is outside this registry
update unless Stefan expands scope.

## Verification and evidence

Each issue first reproduces its user-visible error, then adds a regression that
fails with the producing defect and passes after repair. Tests named below are
planned additions to the indicated existing files or explicitly proposed new
files; they are not claimed to exist or pass today. New proposed files are
 tests/test_contracts.py, tests/test_installed_package.py and
 tests/test_provider_contracts.py. Exact selection names will be set with the tests.
Run focused pytest, Ruff and repository type checks before each scoped commit.
Install pre-commit before committing. Test environments may use python -m pytest
when proving the behavior of a specific installed wheel rather than the checkout.

The local verification matrix must cover all 11 local integrations on a public PDF
and controlled fixtures. Each SDK/service matrix covers all eight service names
and separates offline contracts from authorized live execution. Unsupported Python,
platform, system-tool or model combinations get a reproduced limitation, not a
silent skip counted as success. Test per-extra resolution before advertising a
bundle. A local milestone can close while another integration remains visibly open.

Each closed issue exports source/package/backend identities, exact commands and
fresh results, input/output hashes and limitations to this work unit. Raw parsing
outputs are capability evidence, not benchmark quality scores. No timing ranking
or resource-performance claim is planned.

## Preservation and dependencies

Initial identity/hashes are in baseline/inherited-state.json. Only explicitly
owned paths are staged. Preserve inherited API/module/provider/lockfile bytes in
the review snapshot before integrating them issue by issue. Authorship of these
uncommitted changes is unknown. Secret-bearing prompts, .env, MCP configuration and
credential values are excluded. Add appropriate ignores without removing files.

Public main remains fe66170231dd580d9f919662ed3b89947f091763. The independent
README branch cb5094e6b2690b50da748bcea4875adaa8c74dfc must be fetched and compared
before README editing; reuse its corrections without deleting its branch until
its work actually lands. Public branch pushes are permitted. Merge to main,
package release, service spend and deployment remain separately unauthorized.

### Milestone: `0.3.0.dev0 - Reviewed parser contracts and backend verification`

**Issue 1 - Preserve inherited work and establish failing compatibility evidence**

Files: work-unit evidence, .gitignore, tests/test_api.py and the proposed
 tests/test_contracts.py. Retain an allowlisted, hashed source snapshot and classify
inherited work by chronology and unknown authorship. Ignore credential/runtime
artifacts without deleting them. Add real public-API regressions for the observed
pdf_path keyword break, no-option instance reuse, ignored options and constructor
preset mismatch. Keep these expected failures visible until their owning repairs.
Do not commit inherited provider changes in this checkpoint.

Verify: replay baseline_probe.py against separate installed public/release/working
snapshots. Run uv run --extra dev --extra pypdf --extra pdfplumber pytest
 tests/test_api.py tests/test_contracts.py -q. This checkpoint records the exact
expected failures rather than claiming the suite is green. Mark tests requiring a
later fix as explicit expected failures with the reproduced reason, then remove
that marker in the owning repair; never use an unexplained blanket xfail.
Done when the initial bytes and actual defect triggers are recoverable without
secret files, and inherited work is distinct from owned changes.

### Premises

Input: inherited-source

| Question | Answer | Kind |
|---|---|---|
| record | One allowlisted file revision with bytes and SHA-256; no credential content. | definitional |
| absent-record | A missing historical author remains unknown and does not license deletion. | definitional |
| ordering-spacing | Honor document/page/service order where defined; do not treat filename or registry order as extraction quality. | empirical |
| entity-identity | Git revision plus relative path and byte hash identify a file; timestamps are not authorship. | definitional |
| known-at | Input and effective options are fixed before invocation; SDK/service status is known only when returned and validated. | definitional |
| institution | PDFsmith specification defines wrapper behavior; pinned parser/SDK contracts define provider fields and ordering. | definitional |

### Claims

| Id | Claim | How it could be false |
|---|---|---|
| preserved-source | Inherited source and new changes remain distinguishable. | A new commit absorbs an unrelated modification or a snapshot omits inherited bytes. |

Closes: inherited-source.record, inherited-source.absent-record, inherited-source.ordering-spacing, inherited-source.entity-identity, inherited-source.known-at, inherited-source.institution, preserved-source

branch, required support documentation and tests/test_installed_package.py.
Generate the lockfile using uv, never manually. Build the unpublished wheel,
install it outside the checkout and test declared extras/Python bounds separately.
Use a single development version and record public/released/working identities.

Verify: uv build, installed wheel core-only/each-extra subprocess checks, and all
unpaid tests plus Ruff and repository type checks under declared environments.
Every local working claim needs actual output/input hashes. Every service row
separates SDK-contract verification from a live run. Re-run baseline diagnostics
against the new wheel and show each repaired regression's failing/passing evidence.
Publish no release or public-main merge. Supply one website-editorial-handoff.txt
and concise progress.txt, with exact support/exclusions and live-run protocols.
Done engineering means reviewed offline work and local verification are complete;
full live-service verification remains open until separately authorized runs pass.

### Premises

Input: package-evidence

| Question | Answer | Kind |
|---|---|---|
| record | One installed distribution/backend/configuration test outcome linked to source and artifacts. | definitional |
| absent-record | A skip, missing run or unavailable environment is not a passing integration result. | definitional |
| ordering-spacing | Honor document/page/service order where defined; do not treat filename or registry order as extraction quality. | empirical |
| entity-identity | Wheel hash, source revision, dependency snapshot and input/output hashes identify evidence. | definitional |
| known-at | Input and effective options are fixed before invocation; SDK/service status is known only when returned and validated. | definitional |
| institution | PDFsmith specification defines wrapper behavior; pinned parser/SDK contracts define provider fields and ordering. | definitional |

### Claims

| Id | Claim | How it could be false |
|---|---|---|
| installed-identity | Installable package identity and support claims match fresh executed evidence. | Module and wheel versions disagree, a declared extra omits a dependency or skipped providers are called verified. |

Closes: package-evidence.record, package-evidence.absent-record, package-evidence.ordering-spacing, package-evidence.entity-identity, package-evidence.known-at, package-evidence.institution, installed-identity

## Next actions

Stefan submits the external review packet to GPT and Claude and returns both reports.
Provider spending, public-main merge and package release remain separate decisions.

END FILE plan.md

BEGIN FILE baseline_probe.py SHA256 65ac181f432111f187a6a717a3b8dd48bfbda3dbd3e4e3f5a707b0dd09625d82
"""Record installed-package behavior without contacting parsing services."""

import argparse
import asyncio
import hashlib
import importlib.metadata
import json
import platform
import subprocess
import sys
from pathlib import Path

import pdfsmith
from pdfsmith import available_backends, get_backend, parse, parse_async


def digest(data: bytes) -> str:
    return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()


def main() -> None:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    parser.add_argument("--document", type=Path, required=True)
    parser.add_argument("--fixtures", type=Path, required=True)
    parser.add_argument("--output", type=Path, required=True)
    parser.add_argument("--kind", required=True)
    args = parser.parse_args()
    args.output.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    checks = {}

    def observe(label, operation):
        try:
            value = operation()
            if isinstance(value, str):
                (args.output / f"{label}.txt").write_text(value)
                result = {
                    "outcome": "returned_string",
                    "characters": len(value),
                    "sha256": digest(value.encode()),
                    "summary_heading": "Summary" in value,
                }
            else:
                result = {"outcome": "returned_value", "value": value}
        except Exception as exc:
            result = {
                "outcome": "exception",
                "type": type(exc).__name__,
                "message": str(exc),
            }
        checks[label] = result

    for backend in ("pypdf", "pdfplumber"):
        observe(f"public_{backend}", lambda b=backend: parse(args.document, backend=b))
        observe(
            f"async_{backend}",
            lambda b=backend: asyncio.run(parse_async(args.document, backend=b)),
        )
        for fixture in ("text.pdf", "blank.pdf", "corrupt.pdf", "missing.pdf"):
            observe(
                f"{backend}_{fixture}",
                lambda b=backend, f=fixture: parse(args.fixtures / f, backend=b),
            )
    observe("default", lambda: parse(args.document))
    observe("default_backend", lambda: get_backend().name)
    observe("discovery", lambda: [b.name for b in available_backends()])
    observe("singleton", lambda: get_backend("pypdf") is get_backend("pypdf"))
    observe(
        "keyword_compatibility", lambda: parse(pdf_path=args.document, backend="pypdf")
    )
    observe("unknown_backend", lambda: parse(args.document, backend="not-a-parser"))
    observe("missing_integration", lambda: parse(args.document, backend="docling"))
    observe(
        "layout_preset",
        lambda: parse(args.document, backend="pdfplumber", preset="best"),
    )
    observe(
        "invalid_preset", lambda: parse(args.document, backend="pypdf", preset="typo")
    )
    observe("unsupported_ocr", lambda: parse(args.document, backend="pypdf", ocr=True))
    observe(
        "invalid_option",
        lambda: parse(args.document, backend="pypdf", backend_options={"typo": True}),
    )
    observe("directory_input", lambda: parse(args.fixtures, backend="pypdf"))
    cli = {}
    for label, tail in {
        "version": ["--version"],
        "backends": ["backends"],
        "parse": ["parse", str(args.document), "--backend", "pypdf"],
        "corrupt": ["parse", str(args.fixtures / "corrupt.pdf"), "--backend", "pypdf"],
        "blank": ["parse", str(args.fixtures / "blank.pdf"), "--backend", "pypdf"],
        "unknown": ["parse", str(args.document), "--backend", "not-a-parser"],
        "missing": ["parse", str(args.fixtures / "missing.pdf"), "--backend", "pypdf"],
        "options": [
            "parse",
            str(args.document),
            "--backend",
            "pdfplumber",
            "--preset",
            "best",
        ],
    }.items():
        command = [str(Path(sys.executable).with_name("pdfsmith")), *tail]
        cli_result = subprocess.run(command, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=60)
        for channel in ("stdout", "stderr"):
            (args.output / f"cli_{label}_{channel}.txt").write_text(
                getattr(cli_result, channel)
            )
        cli[label] = {
            "command": command,
            "returncode": cli_result.returncode,
            "stdout_sha256": digest(cli_result.stdout.encode()),
            "stderr_sha256": digest(cli_result.stderr.encode()),
        }
    package = Path(pdfsmith.__file__).parent
    result = {
        "kind": args.kind,
        "python": platform.python_version(),
        "distribution_version": importlib.metadata.version("pdfsmith"),
        "module_version": pdfsmith.__version__,
        "package_path": str(package),
        "module_hashes": {
            str(p.relative_to(package)): digest(p.read_bytes())
            for p in sorted(package.rglob("*.py"))
        },
        "dependencies": {
            d.metadata["Name"]: d.version for d in importlib.metadata.distributions()
        },
        "input_sha256": digest(args.document.read_bytes()),
        "fixture_sha256": {
            p.name: digest(p.read_bytes()) for p in args.fixtures.glob("*.pdf")
        },
        "checks": checks,
        "cli": cli,
        "boundary": "Local capability only; no provider calls or quality comparison.",
    }
    (args.output / "results.json").write_text(json.dumps(result, indent=2) + "\n")
    print(
        json.dumps(
            {
                "kind": args.kind,
                "versions": [result["distribution_version"], result["module_version"]],
                "checks": {
                    k: {
                        a: v
                        for a, v in c.items()
                        if a in {"outcome", "type", "value", "characters"}
                    }
                    for k, c in checks.items()
                },
            },
            indent=2,
        )
    )


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

END FILE baseline_probe.py

BEGIN FILE reproduce-baseline.sh SHA256 a6a91c82367e530eb78b88f8470a1612f2055263c1e5ce00cb35e233abb09538
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

work_dir="$(cd -- "$(dirname -- "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
document="${1:-/home/stefan/agents/document-evidence/data/raw/AK_38344_38344AK1060001-01.pdf}"
mkdir -p "$work_dir/runtime"
replay_dir="$(mktemp -d "$work_dir/runtime/replay-XXXXXX")"

python3 - "$work_dir" "$replay_dir" "$document" <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
import hashlib, json, sys, tarfile, urllib.request
work, replay, document = map(Path, sys.argv[1:])
expected = 'd93b3432fee068291117ba8d057233c5e1e70a7415be3052f88ccb5204872e80'
assert hashlib.sha256(document.read_bytes()).hexdigest() == expected, 'Input differs'
with tarfile.open(work / 'initial-source.tar.gz') as archive:
    archive.extractall(replay, filter='data')
identity = json.loads((work / 'baseline/release-identity.json').read_text())
wheel = urllib.request.urlopen(identity['url'], timeout=60).read()
assert hashlib.sha256(wheel).hexdigest() == identity['sha256'], 'Wheel differs'
(replay / identity['filename']).write_bytes(wheel)
PY

for install_kind in public working release; do
  uv venv --python 3.12 "$replay_dir/$install_kind-env"
  package_path="$replay_dir/$install_kind-source"
  if [[ "$install_kind" == release ]]; then
    package_path="$replay_dir/pdfsmith-0.2.0-py3-none-any.whl"
  fi
  uv pip install --python "$replay_dir/$install_kind-env/bin/python" \
    "$package_path" -r "$work_dir/baseline/requirements.txt"
done

"$replay_dir/public-env/bin/python" - "$replay_dir" <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
import sys
from reportlab.pdfgen import canvas
fixtures = Path(sys.argv[1]) / 'fixtures'
fixtures.mkdir()
c = canvas.Canvas(str(fixtures / 'text.pdf'), invariant=1)
c.drawString(72, 720, 'PDFsmith controlled fixture')
c.drawString(72, 690, 'Invoice PS-1042 Total USD 123.45')
c.save()
c = canvas.Canvas(str(fixtures / 'blank.pdf'), invariant=1)
c.showPage()
c.save()
(fixtures / 'corrupt.pdf').write_bytes(b'%PDF-1.7\ncorrupt control\n')
PY

python3 - "$work_dir" "$replay_dir" "$document" <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
import json, os, subprocess, sys
work, replay, document = map(Path, sys.argv[1:])
env = {k: v for k, v in os.environ.items()
       if k in {'PATH', 'LANG', 'LC_ALL', 'SSL_CERT_FILE', 'SSL_CERT_DIR'}}
(replay / 'home').mkdir()
env.update(HOME=str(replay / 'home'), PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE='1')
for kind in ('public', 'working', 'release'):
    command = [str(replay / (kind + '-env') / 'bin/python'),
               str(work / 'baseline_probe.py'), '--document', str(document),
               '--fixtures', str(replay / 'fixtures'), '--output',
               str(replay / 'outputs' / kind), '--kind', kind]
    result = subprocess.run(command, cwd=replay, env=env, capture_output=True,
                            text=True, timeout=180, check=True)
    (replay / (kind + '-probe.log')).write_text(result.stdout + result.stderr)
    fresh = json.loads((replay / 'outputs' / kind / 'results.json').read_text())
    old = json.loads((work / 'baseline' / (kind + '.json')).read_text())
    assert fresh['module_hashes'] == old['module_hashes'], kind + ': implementation drift'
    def stable_checks(checks):
        return {k: {field: value for field, value in row.items() if field != 'message'}
                for k, row in checks.items()}
    assert stable_checks(fresh['checks']) == stable_checks(old['checks']), kind + ': behavior differs'
    for label, outcome in old['cli'].items():
        assert fresh['cli'][label]['returncode'] == outcome['returncode'], label
        assert fresh['cli'][label]['stdout_sha256'] == outcome['stdout_sha256'], label
    assert fresh['fixture_sha256'] == old['fixture_sha256'], kind + ': fixture differs'
    print(kind + ': reproduced baseline observations and hashes')
print('Replay artifacts: ' + str(replay))
PY

END FILE reproduce-baseline.sh

BEGIN FILE public-source/src/pdfsmith/__init__.py SHA256 56d146feaa3dfafe944c54ce91a5d6be4b7ebae5faa7e4e34d80e2bbe6bf5247
"""
pdfsmith - PDF to Markdown conversion with multiple backend support.

A unified interface to 10+ PDF parsing libraries. Pick the right tool for the job,
or let pdfsmith choose for you.

Basic usage:
    from pdfsmith import parse

    # Auto-select best available backend
    markdown = parse("document.pdf")

    # Use specific backend
    markdown = parse("document.pdf", backend="docling")

    # List available backends
    from pdfsmith import available_backends
    print(available_backends())
"""

from pdfsmith.api import available_backends, get_backend, parse, parse_async

__version__ = "0.2.0"
__all__ = ["parse", "parse_async", "available_backends", "get_backend", "__version__"]

END FILE public-source/src/pdfsmith/__init__.py

BEGIN FILE public-source/src/pdfsmith/api.py SHA256 652d6f3f532513295196b36bd34e0535259cbcf728b9935ecf1a60976f23154a
"""
Core API for pdfsmith.

Provides a simple interface to parse PDFs to markdown using various backends.
"""

from pathlib import Path
from typing import Literal

from pdfsmith.backends.registry import BACKEND_REGISTRY, BackendInfo

# Backend preference order (best first, considering quality vs availability)
DEFAULT_PREFERENCE = [
    "docling",  # Best quality, heavy
    "marker",  # Great for academic docs
    "pymupdf4llm",  # Good balance
    "kreuzberg",  # Fast, good quality
    "unstructured",  # Versatile
    "pdfplumber",  # Reliable for tables
    "pymupdf",  # Fast, basic
    "pypdf",  # Lightweight fallback
    "pdfminer",  # Legacy but works
    "pypdfium2",  # Alternative
    "extractous",  # Rust-based
]

BackendName = Literal[
    "docling",
    "marker",
    "pymupdf4llm",
    "kreuzberg",
    "unstructured",
    "pdfplumber",
    "pymupdf",
    "pypdf",
    "pdfminer",
    "pypdfium2",
    "extractous",
]


def available_backends() -> list[BackendInfo]:
    """Return list of available (installed) backends with their info."""
    available = []
    for name in DEFAULT_PREFERENCE:
        if name in BACKEND_REGISTRY:
            info = BACKEND_REGISTRY[name]
            if info.is_available():
                available.append(info)
    return available


def get_backend(name: BackendName | None = None):
    """
    Get a backend instance by name, or auto-select the best available.

    Args:
        name: Backend name, or None to auto-select

    Returns:
        Backend instance ready to parse

    Raises:
        ImportError: If specified backend is not installed
        RuntimeError: If no backends are available
    """
    if name is not None:
        if name not in BACKEND_REGISTRY:
            available = list(BACKEND_REGISTRY.keys())
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown backend: {name}. Available: {available}")
        info = BACKEND_REGISTRY[name]
        if not info.is_available():
            raise ImportError(
                f"Backend '{name}' is not installed. "
                f"Install with: pip install pdfsmith[{name}]"
            )
        return info.get_instance()

    # Auto-select best available
    for backend_name in DEFAULT_PREFERENCE:
        if backend_name in BACKEND_REGISTRY:
            info = BACKEND_REGISTRY[backend_name]
            if info.is_available():
                return info.get_instance()

    raise RuntimeError(
        "No PDF parsing backends are installed. "
        "Install with: pip install pdfsmith[light] or pdfsmith[recommended]"
    )


def parse(
    pdf_path: str | Path,
    *,
    backend: BackendName | None = None,
) -> str:
    """
    Parse a PDF file to markdown.

    Args:
        pdf_path: Path to the PDF file
        backend: Backend to use, or None to auto-select best available

    Returns:
        Markdown string extracted from the PDF

    Examples:
        # Auto-select backend
        markdown = parse("document.pdf")

        # Use specific backend
        markdown = parse("document.pdf", backend="docling")
    """
    pdf_path = Path(pdf_path)
    if not pdf_path.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError(f"PDF file not found: {pdf_path}")

    backend_instance = get_backend(backend)
    return backend_instance.parse(pdf_path)


async def parse_async(
    pdf_path: str | Path,
    *,
    backend: BackendName | None = None,
) -> str:
    """
    Parse a PDF file to markdown asynchronously.

    Args:
        pdf_path: Path to the PDF file
        backend: Backend to use, or None to auto-select best available

    Returns:
        Markdown string extracted from the PDF
    """
    pdf_path = Path(pdf_path)
    if not pdf_path.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError(f"PDF file not found: {pdf_path}")

    backend_instance = get_backend(backend)

    # Use async method if available, otherwise run sync in executor
    if hasattr(backend_instance, "parse_async"):
        return await backend_instance.parse_async(pdf_path)
    else:
        import asyncio

        loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
        return await loop.run_in_executor(None, backend_instance.parse, pdf_path)

END FILE public-source/src/pdfsmith/api.py

BEGIN FILE public-source/src/pdfsmith/backends/registry.py SHA256 305868bb01930fc5940ce66d8f2580c29d7eb064768fab592ac4d4dd4cb10e17
"""
Backend registry with lazy loading.

Each backend is only imported when actually used, keeping the base package lightweight.
"""

from collections.abc import Callable
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any


@dataclass
class BackendInfo:
    """Information about a parsing backend."""

    name: str
    description: str
    package: str  # PyPI package name for installation
    weight: str  # "light", "medium", "heavy"
    loader: Callable[[], Any]  # Function to load the backend class

    _instance: Any = None
    _available: bool | None = None

    def is_available(self) -> bool:
        """Check if this backend's dependencies are installed."""
        if self._available is None:
            try:
                backend_class = self.loader()
                # Check the AVAILABLE flag if it exists
                import importlib

                mod = importlib.import_module(backend_class.__module__)
                self._available = getattr(mod, "AVAILABLE", True)
            except ImportError:
                self._available = False
        return self._available

    def get_instance(self):
        """Get or create a backend instance."""
        if self._instance is None:
            backend_class = self.loader()
            self._instance = backend_class()
        return self._instance


class BaseBackend:
    """Base class for all backends."""

    name: str = "base"

    def parse(self, pdf_path: Path) -> str:
        """Parse PDF to markdown."""
        raise NotImplementedError


def _load_pypdf():
    from pdfsmith.backends.pypdf_backend import PyPDFBackend

    return PyPDFBackend


def _load_pdfplumber():
    from pdfsmith.backends.pdfplumber_backend import PDFPlumberBackend

    return PDFPlumberBackend

    return DatabricksBackend


def _load_llamaparse():
    from pdfsmith.backends.llamaparse_backend import LlamaParseBackend

    return LlamaParseBackend

    from pdfsmith.backends.anthropic_backend import AnthropicBackend

    return AnthropicBackend

    from pdfsmith.backends.openai_backend import OpenAIBackend

    return OpenAIBackend


def _load_gemini():
    from pdfsmith.backends.gemini_backend import GeminiBackend

    return GeminiBackend

BEGIN FILE working-source/src/pdfsmith/__init__.py SHA256 4ae92d4bf4672042d0a9d556e9368416fb7b3a3c24643b441d08ddf5361b2c1f
"""
pdfsmith - PDF to Markdown conversion with multiple backend support.

A unified interface to 19 PDF/image parsing backends. Pick the right tool for the job,
or let pdfsmith choose for you.

Basic usage:
    from pdfsmith import parse

    # Auto-select best available backend
    markdown = parse("document.pdf")

    # Use specific backend
    markdown = parse("document.pdf", backend="docling")

    # Use quality preset
    markdown = parse("document.pdf", preset="best")

    # Enable OCR with language
    markdown = parse("scan.pdf", ocr="eng+deu")

    # Backend-specific options
    markdown = parse("document.pdf", backend="docling",
                    backend_options={"do_table_structure": True})

    # Parse images (auto-selects image-capable backend)
    markdown = parse("scan.png")

    # List available backends
    from pdfsmith import available_backends
    print(available_backends())
"""

from pdfsmith.api import (
    IMAGE_CAPABLE_BACKENDS,
    PRESETS,
    BackendName,
    PresetName,
    available_backends,
    get_backend,
    parse,
    parse_async,
)

__version__ = "0.3.0"
__all__ = [
    "parse",
    "parse_async",
    "available_backends",
    "get_backend",
    "BackendName",
    "PresetName",
    "PRESETS",
    "IMAGE_CAPABLE_BACKENDS",
    "__version__",
]

END FILE working-source/src/pdfsmith/__init__.py

BEGIN FILE working-source/src/pdfsmith/api.py SHA256 257ad38a157aca31a2166bc714e208ed0eee6a5446a67bc6beacf13dc8e2440a
"""
Core API for pdfsmith.

Provides a simple interface to parse PDFs and images to markdown using various backends.

API Levels:
    Level 1 (Simple): parse("document.pdf") - auto-select backend, defaults
    Level 2 (Common): parse("document.pdf", preset="best", ocr=True) - cross-backend options
    Level 3 (Advanced): parse("document.pdf", backend_options={...}) - backend-specific

Configuration Sources (in order of precedence):
    1. Explicit API arguments (preset, ocr, backend_options)
    2. Environment variables (PDFSMITH_<BACKEND>_<OPTION>)
    3. Config files (.pdfsmith/<backend>.yaml, ~/.config/pdfsmith/<backend>.yaml)
    4. Built-in defaults
"""

from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Literal

from pdfsmith.backends.registry import BACKEND_REGISTRY, BackendInfo

# Backend preference order (best first, considering quality vs availability)
DEFAULT_PREFERENCE = [
    "docling",  # Best quality, heavy
    "marker",  # Great for academic docs
    "pymupdf4llm",  # Good balance
    "kreuzberg",  # Fast, good quality
    "unstructured",  # Versatile
    "pdfplumber",  # Reliable for tables
    "pymupdf",  # Fast, basic
    "pypdf",  # Lightweight fallback
    "pdfminer",  # Legacy but works
    "pypdfium2",  # Alternative
    "extractous",  # Rust-based
]

# Image preference order (for image inputs)
IMAGE_PREFERENCE = [
    "docling",  # InputFormat.IMAGE support
    "kreuzberg",  # Native image OCR
    "extractous",  # Tesseract-based
]

# Backends that support image input
IMAGE_CAPABLE_BACKENDS = {
    "docling",
    "kreuzberg",
    "extractous",
    # Commercial APIs (all support images)
    "aws_textract",
    "azure_document_intelligence",
    "google_document_ai",
    "databricks",
    "llamaparse",
    # LLM vision APIs
    "anthropic",
    "openai",
    "gemini",
}

# Supported image extensions
IMAGE_EXTENSIONS = {".png", ".jpg", ".jpeg", ".tiff", ".webp", ".bmp"}

BackendName = Literal[
    # Open source
    "docling",
    "marker",
    "pymupdf4llm",
    "kreuzberg",
    "unstructured",
    "pdfplumber",
    "pymupdf",
    "pypdf",
    "pdfminer",
    "pypdfium2",
    "extractous",
    # Commercial
    "aws_textract",
    "azure_document_intelligence",
    "google_document_ai",
    "databricks",
    "llamaparse",
    # LLM Vision
    "anthropic",
    "openai",
    "gemini",
]

PresetName = Literal["fast", "balanced", "best"]

# Preset configurations per backend
# These are applied when a preset is specified
PRESETS: dict[str, dict[str, dict[str, Any]]] = {
    "fast": {
        "docling": {"do_ocr": False, "do_table_structure": False},
        "marker": {"force_ocr": False, "use_llm": False},
        "unstructured": {"strategy": "fast"},
        "pdfplumber": {"layout": False},
        "pdfminer": {"boxes_flow": None},
        "kreuzberg": {"force_ocr": False},
    },
    "balanced": {
        "docling": {"do_ocr": False, "do_table_structure": True},
        "marker": {"force_ocr": False, "use_llm": False},
        "unstructured": {"strategy": "auto"},
        "pdfplumber": {"layout": False},
        "pdfminer": {"boxes_flow": 0.5},
        "kreuzberg": {"force_ocr": False},
    },
    "best": {
        "docling": {"do_ocr": True, "do_table_structure": True},
        "marker": {"force_ocr": True, "use_llm": True},
        "unstructured": {"strategy": "hi_res", "infer_table_structure": True},
        "pdfplumber": {"layout": True},
        "pdfminer": {"boxes_flow": 0.5, "detect_vertical": True, "all_texts": True},
        "kreuzberg": {"force_ocr": True},
    },
}


def available_backends() -> list[BackendInfo]:
    """Return list of available (installed) backends with their info."""
    available = []
    for name in DEFAULT_PREFERENCE:
        if name in BACKEND_REGISTRY:
            info = BACKEND_REGISTRY[name]
            if info.is_available():
                available.append(info)
    return available


def get_backend(
    name: BackendName | None = None,
    *,
    backend_options: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
    for_image: bool = False,
):
    """
    Get a backend instance by name, or auto-select the best available.

    Args:
        name: Backend name, or None to auto-select
        backend_options: Options to pass to backend constructor
        for_image: If True, only consider image-capable backends for auto-select

    Returns:
        Backend instance ready to parse

    Raises:
        ImportError: If specified backend is not installed
        RuntimeError: If no backends are available
        ValueError: If backend doesn't support images but for_image=True
    """
    backend_options = backend_options or {}

    if name is not None:
        if name not in BACKEND_REGISTRY:
            available = list(BACKEND_REGISTRY.keys())
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown backend: {name}. Available: {available}")

        # Check image support
        if for_image and name not in IMAGE_CAPABLE_BACKENDS:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Backend '{name}' does not support image inputs. "
                f"Image-capable backends: {sorted(IMAGE_CAPABLE_BACKENDS)}"
            )

        info = BACKEND_REGISTRY[name]
        if not info.is_available():
            raise ImportError(
                f"Backend '{name}' is not installed. "
                f"Install with: pip install pdfsmith[{name}]"
            )

        # Create instance with options
        backend_class = info.loader()
        return backend_class(**backend_options)

    # Auto-select best available
    preference = IMAGE_PREFERENCE if for_image else DEFAULT_PREFERENCE

    for backend_name in preference:
        if backend_name in BACKEND_REGISTRY:
            # Skip non-image backends when for_image
            if for_image and backend_name not in IMAGE_CAPABLE_BACKENDS:
                continue

            info = BACKEND_REGISTRY[backend_name]
            if info.is_available():
                backend_class = info.loader()
                return backend_class(**backend_options)

    if for_image:
        raise RuntimeError(
            "No image-capable backends are installed. "
            "Install with: pip install pdfsmith[docling] or pdfsmith[kreuzberg]"
        )
    raise RuntimeError(
        "No PDF parsing backends are installed. "
        "Install with: pip install pdfsmith[light] or pdfsmith[recommended]"
    )


def _build_backend_options(
    backend_name: str,
    preset: PresetName | None,
    ocr: bool | str | None,
    backend_options: dict[str, Any] | None,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Build merged backend options from preset, common params, and explicit options.

    Priority (highest to lowest):
        1. Explicit backend_options
        2. Common options (ocr)
        3. Preset options
    """
    merged: dict[str, Any] = {}

    # 1. Apply preset if specified
    if preset and backend_name in PRESETS.get(preset, {}):
        merged.update(PRESETS[preset][backend_name])

    # 2. Apply common OCR option (translate to backend-specific key)
    if ocr is not None:
        # Map common 'ocr' param to backend-specific option
        ocr_mappings = {
            "docling": "do_ocr",
            "marker": "force_ocr",
            "kreuzberg": "force_ocr",
            "unstructured": "ocr_languages",  # Triggers OCR if non-empty
        }
        if backend_name in ocr_mappings:
            key = ocr_mappings[backend_name]
            if isinstance(ocr, bool):
                merged[key] = ocr
            elif isinstance(ocr, str):
                # OCR with languages (e.g., "eng+deu")
                merged[key] = True
                if backend_name == "docling":
                    merged["ocr_languages"] = ocr.split("+")
                elif backend_name in ("kreuzberg", "unstructured"):
                    merged["ocr_languages"] = ocr

    # 3. Override with explicit backend_options
    if backend_options:
        merged.update(backend_options)

    return merged


def parse(
    input_path: str | Path,
    *,
    backend: BackendName | None = None,
    preset: PresetName | None = None,
    ocr: bool | str | None = None,
    backend_options: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
) -> str:
    """
    Parse a PDF or image file to markdown.

    This is the main entry point for pdfsmith. It supports three levels of usage:

    Level 1 - Simple (defaults):
        markdown = parse("document.pdf")

    Level 2 - Common options (cross-backend):
        markdown = parse("document.pdf", preset="best", ocr=True)

    Level 3 - Backend-specific options:
        markdown = parse("document.pdf", backend="docling",
                        backend_options={"do_table_structure": True, "device": "cuda"})

    Args:
        input_path: Path to the input file (PDF or image)
        backend: Backend to use, or None to auto-select best available
        preset: Quality preset ("fast", "balanced", "best"). Affects multiple backends.
        ocr: Enable OCR. True/False or language string like "eng+deu".
        backend_options: Backend-specific options (highest priority)

    Returns:
        Markdown string extracted from the document

    Examples:
        # Auto-select backend
        markdown = parse("document.pdf")

        # Use specific backend
        markdown = parse("document.pdf", backend="docling")

        # Use preset for quality/speed tradeoff
        markdown = parse("document.pdf", preset="best")

        # Enable OCR with specific languages
        markdown = parse("scan.pdf", ocr="eng+deu")

        # Full control with backend-specific options
        markdown = parse("document.pdf", backend="docling",
                        backend_options={"do_ocr": True, "device": "cuda"})

        # Parse image files (auto-selects image-capable backend)
        markdown = parse("scan.png")
    """
    input_path = Path(input_path)
    if not input_path.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError(f"Input file not found: {input_path}")

    # Detect input type
    suffix = input_path.suffix.lower()
    is_image = suffix in IMAGE_EXTENSIONS

    # Determine backend name for option building
    backend_name = backend
    if backend_name is None:
        # Peek at which backend will be selected
        preference = IMAGE_PREFERENCE if is_image else DEFAULT_PREFERENCE
        for name in preference:
            if name in BACKEND_REGISTRY and BACKEND_REGISTRY[name].is_available():
                if not is_image or name in IMAGE_CAPABLE_BACKENDS:
                    backend_name = name
                    break

    # Build merged options
    merged_options = _build_backend_options(
        backend_name or "docling",  # Fallback for option building
        preset,
        ocr,
        backend_options,
    )

    # Get backend instance with options
    backend_instance = get_backend(backend, backend_options=merged_options, for_image=is_image)
    return backend_instance.parse(input_path)


async def parse_async(
    input_path: str | Path,
    *,
    backend: BackendName | None = None,
    preset: PresetName | None = None,
    ocr: bool | str | None = None,
    backend_options: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
) -> str:
    """
    Parse a PDF or image file to markdown asynchronously.

    See parse() for full documentation of arguments and examples.

    Args:
        input_path: Path to the input file (PDF or image)
        backend: Backend to use, or None to auto-select best available
        preset: Quality preset ("fast", "balanced", "best")
        ocr: Enable OCR. True/False or language string like "eng+deu"
        backend_options: Backend-specific options

    Returns:
        Markdown string extracted from the document
    """
    input_path = Path(input_path)
    if not input_path.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError(f"Input file not found: {input_path}")

    # Detect input type
    suffix = input_path.suffix.lower()
    is_image = suffix in IMAGE_EXTENSIONS

    # Determine backend name for option building
    backend_name = backend
    if backend_name is None:
        preference = IMAGE_PREFERENCE if is_image else DEFAULT_PREFERENCE
        for name in preference:
            if name in BACKEND_REGISTRY and BACKEND_REGISTRY[name].is_available():
                if not is_image or name in IMAGE_CAPABLE_BACKENDS:
                    backend_name = name
                    break

    # Build merged options
    merged_options = _build_backend_options(
        backend_name or "docling",
        preset,
        ocr,
        backend_options,
    )

    # Get backend instance
    backend_instance = get_backend(backend, backend_options=merged_options, for_image=is_image)

    # Use async method if available, otherwise run sync in executor
    if hasattr(backend_instance, "parse_async"):
        return await backend_instance.parse_async(input_path)
    else:
        import asyncio

        loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
        return await loop.run_in_executor(None, backend_instance.parse, input_path)

END FILE working-source/src/pdfsmith/api.py

"""Backend implementations for pdfsmith."""

from pdfsmith.backends.registry import BACKEND_REGISTRY, BackendInfo

__all__ = ["BACKEND_REGISTRY", "BackendInfo"]
